Let's analyze previous algorithm weaknesses:
- Subgradient with top-k active peaks; momentum; coordinate bisection; pair swaps. It probably gets stuck.
- We can improve with:
  1. Smooth max with adaptive β and gradient descent, not just top-k.
  2. Coordinate descent exact-ish with candidate intersections (no FFT per coordinate).
  3. Frequency-based global seeds: random low-pass filtered anti-symmetric sequences; sinusoidal combinations; polynomial fits.
  4. Multi-start over lengths, maybe 768,1024,1536.
  5. Use evaluate_sequence occasionally to prune.
  6. Use warm-start from parent interpolated.

Need ensure code doesn't time out. Time budget 1800. We can allocate ~1700 for search. Need avoid too long per output. We cannot actually test, so code must be efficient and robust.

Let's design concrete functions:

`fast_convolve(a)`:
```
def fast_convolve(a):
    n = len(a)
    if n <= 64:
        return np.convolve(a, a)
    size = 2*n - 1
    fft_size = 1 << (size - 1).bit_length()
    A = np.fft.rfft(a.astype(np.float64), fft_size)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A * A, fft_size)
    return c[:size].real
```
Potential numerical negative small; for max okay.

`convolve2(a,b)` similar.

`project_simplex(v)`:
Use standard. Need handle large n. For v real.

`project_pair_complement(v, n)` for pair sum manifold:
```
def project_complement(v):
    n=len(v)
    a=np.empty(n)
    for i in range(n//2):
       j=n-1-i
       s=np.clip(n*(v[i]-v[j]), -1.0, 1.0)
       a[i]=(1+s)/n
       a[j]=(1-s)/n
    if n%2: a[n//2]=1.0/n
    # sum exactly 1? due clip still each pair sum 2/n, yes.
    return a
```
But this projection of a gradient step may lose scale? It assumes output within family. Could use as a phase: start from current a, project to family, then optimize smoothing with projection to this family. However project_complement as Euclidean projection onto family? It forces each pair sum to 2/n, but may not be best if current has pair sum differences; okay for phase.

For gradient in s family, we can use project_simplex? If using full a, then project back to family after each step: a = project_complement(a + step*grad). This keeps in family.

Smooth max gradient function:
```
def smooth_gradient(a, beta):
    conv = fast_convolve(a)
    # stable weights
    b = np.maximum(conv, 0?)? For softmax, negative okay. Use conv.
    if beta <= 0: ...
    maxb = np.max(conv)
    if maxb > 0:
       ex = np.exp(beta*(conv - maxb))
    else: ex = np.exp(beta*conv)
    w = ex / ex.sum()
    # grad via convolve2(a, w[::-1])
    L=2*n-1
    c = convolve2(a, w[::-1])
    grad = 2.0 * c[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]
    return grad, conv, maxb
```

Need build A_ub efficiently:
```
def build_conv_matrix(f, n):
    L=2*n-1
    # rows k, cols i: f[k-i] if 0<=k-i<n
    k = np.arange(L)[:, None]
    i = np.arange(n)[None, :]
    idx = k - i
    mask = (idx >= 0) & (idx < n)
    A = np.zeros((L,n))
    A[mask] = f[idx[mask]]
    return A
```
For n=1024, L=2047, A 4.2M (33MB), okay. For n=2048, 16M (128MB) maybe okay but LP slow. Limit n<=1200.
LP:
```
def lp_direction(f, M):
    n=len(f); A=build; c=-np.ones(n)
    res=linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=np.full(L,M), bounds=(0,None), method='highs', options={'presolve':True})
    if res.success: return res.x
```

Let's write functions:

```
def normalize_seq(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.where(a > 0, a, 0.0)
    s = a.sum()
    if s <= 1e-15:
        return np.ones(len(a))/len(a)
    return a/s
```

`smooth_gradient` as above. Need `convolve2` maybe use `fast_convolve` if same? For a and w. Define:
```
def conv_filter(a, b):
    # full linear convolution of real arrays
    na, nb = len(a), len(nb)
    size=na+nb-1
    fft_size=1 << (size-1).bit_length()
    A=np.fft.rfft(a, fft_size)
    B=np.fft.rfft(b, fft_size)
    return np.fft.irfft(A*B, fft_size)[:size]
```
For smooth grad, `c=conv_filter(a, w[::-1])`.

`coordinate_descent_pass`:
Need be careful with variable names and no lambdas.
Pseudo:
```
def coordinate_descent_pass(a, conv, max_time=30.0, grid_points=18, top_k=10, refine_points=8):
    n=len(a); L=2*n-1
    a = a.copy()
    a = normalize_seq(a)
    conv = fast_convolve(a)
    best_obj = conv.max()
    best_a = a.copy()
    t0=time.monotonic()
    improved_total=False
    for j in range(n):
       if time.monotonic()-t0 > max_time: break
       xold=a[j]
       s=1.0-xold
       if s < 1e-14:
           continue
       b=a.copy(); b[j]=0.0; b = b/s
       D=np.zeros(L)
       st=j; en=min(j+n, L)
       D[st:en]=b[:en-st]
       E=np.zeros(L)
       if 2*j < L: E[2*j]=1.0
       B=(conv - xold*xold*E - 2.0*xold*s*D)/(s*s)
       # clean
       # maybe set very small negative to 0
       B = np.where(B > -1e-12, B, 0.0)  # but if large negative due error? use max(0)
       # coeff arrays
       Acoeff = E - 2.0*B
       Bcoeff = 2.0*(D - B)
       Ccoeff = B
       # candidates
       xs = np.empty(...)
       add 0,1,xold grid
       # top constraints from conv (values at xold are conv)
       K=min(top_k, L)
       top = np.argpartition(conv, -K)[-K:] if K<L else np.arange(L)
       for p in top:
          # derivative root
          if abs(Acoeff[p])>1e-15:
             r = -Bcoeff[p]/(2.0*Acoeff[p])
             add if 0<=r<=1
          for q in top after p:
             da=Acoeff[p]-Acoeff[q]; db=Bcoeff[p]-Bcoeff[q]; dc=Ccoeff[p]-Ccoeff[q]
             if abs(da)>1e-14:
                disc=db*db-4*da*dc
                if disc>=0: roots...
             elif abs(db)>1e-14:
                root=-dc/db; add
       # unique
       xs = np.unique(xs[(xs>=0)&(xs<=1)])
       # maybe limit to 36, if too many keep grid around old and top intersections? Simpler: sort by proximity to xold? We can cap:
       if len(xs)>max_candidates:
          # always include 0,1,xold, best? We'll choose by heuristic: take max_candidates points: grid + closest intersections.
          # For simplicity, take first max_candidates after sorting unique; but may drop important. Better: create grid unique then add intersections until cap.
       # Evaluate
       xs = np.clip(xs,0,1)
       one_x = 1.0 - xs
       # To save memory, if len(xs)*L > 2e6, split chunks maybe.
       best_val=np.inf; best_x=xold
       for chunk xs:
          vals = (one_x**2)[:,None]*B + 2.0*xs*one_x[:,None]*D + (xs**2)[:,None]*E
          m = vals.max(axis=1)
          idx = m.argmin()
          if m[idx] < best_val: best_val=m[idx]; best_x=xs[idx]
       # optional local refine around best_x: sample small interval [best_x ± step] with 16 points if best_val improved
       if best_val < conv.max() - 1e-12:
           # apply
           a_new = a.copy()
           if best_x == 0: a_new[j]=0
           elif best_x >= 1-1e-15: a_new[:]=0; a_new[j]=1
           else:
              a_new[j]=best_x
              others = a.copy(); others[j]=0
              a_new = best_x*np.zeros(n)? Let's do:
              a_new = a_new / s for others? Actually old a_new currently a. Do:
              a_new = a.copy()
              a_new[j]=best_x
              if best_x <1:
                 factor=(1.0-best_x)/s
                 mask = np.ones(n,bool); mask[j]=False
                 a_new[mask] *= factor
              a_new = np.maximum(a_new,0)
              sn=a_new.sum(); if sn>0: a_new/=sn
           conv_new = (1-best_x)**2*B + 2*best_x*(1-best_x)*D + best_x*best_x*E
           conv_new = np.where(conv_new > -1e-12, conv_new, 0.0) # but max maybe
           if conv_new.max() < best_obj - 1e-14:
              best_obj=conv_new.max(); best_a=a_new.copy()
           a=a_new; conv=conv_new
           improved_total=True
       # if no improve for this j, no action
    # after pass, recompute to correct
    a=normalize_seq(best_a)
    conv=fast_convolve(a)
    return a, float(conv.max())
```

`transfer_polish(a, conv, time_limit, m_grid=20, n_trials=None)`:

def transfer_polish(a, conv, max_time=30.0, grid_points=20, rng_seed=123):
    a=normalize_seq(a); conv=fast_convolve(a); best_obj=conv.max(); best_a=a.copy()
    n=len(a); L=2*n-1; t0=time.monotonic(); rng=np.random.default_rng(rng_seed); trials=0
    while time...:
       trials+=1
       i=rng.integers(0,n); j=rng.integers(0,n)
       if i==j: continue
       xi=a[i]; xj=a[j]
       if xi + xj < 1e-15: continue
       # direction v=e_i - e_j? Let's use increase i, decrease j: δ∈[-xj, xi]
       # C = shift(i) - shift(j) (conv with e_i-e_j)
       C=np.zeros(L)
       # shift a by i: C[i:i+n]=a
       C[i:i+n]=a
       # subtract shift j: C[j:j+n]-=a
       # E = delta(2i) -2 delta(i+j) + delta(2j)
       E=np.zeros(L)
       if 2*i<L: E[2*i]+=1
       if i+j<L: E[i+j]-=2
       if 2*j<L: E[2*j]+=1
       # a' = a + δ (e_i - e_j)
       lo=-xj; hi=xi
       if hi <= lo + 1e-15: continue
       xs = np.linspace(lo, hi, grid_points)
       # Evaluate chunks
       best_x=0; best_val=best_obj
       for chunk:
          vals = conv + 2.0*xs[:,None]*C + xs[:,None]*xs[:,None]*E
          m=vals.max(axis=1)
          # Need ensure nonneg: endpoints already.
          idx=argmin
       if m_best < best_obj - 1e-12:
          a[i]+=best_x; a[j]-=best_x; clip; normalize maybe sum preserved
          conv_new = conv + 2*best_x*C + best_x*best_x*E; clip
          best_obj=conv_new.max(); best_a=a.copy()
          a=a_new; conv=conv_new
       # every trial maybe if time
    return best_a,best_obj
```

def suggested_pairs(a, conv, count=50):
   n=len(a); pairs=set()
   top_k = np.argpartition(conv, -20)[-20:]
   for k in top_k:
       js = np.arange(max(0,k-n+1), min(n-1,k)+1)
       vals = a[js]
       if len(js)>1:
          q_idx = js[vals.argmax()]
          p_idx = js[vals.argmin()]
          if p_idx!=q_idx: pairs.add((p_idx,q_idx))
   # also random

Let's detail each.

`fast_convolve`:
```
def fast_convolve(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = len(a)
    if n == 0: return np.array([])
    if n <= 128:
        return np.convolve(a, a)
    size = 2*n - 1
    fft_size = 1 << (size - 1).bit_length()
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, fft_size)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A * A, fft_size)
    return c[:size].real
```
Using direct for small reduces error.

`conv_filter` similar but if b length maybe L. Use fft_size based on len a + len b -1. For grad, len a=n, len w=L => size=3n-2. Next pow2. Fine.

`smooth_gradient(a, beta)`:
```
def smooth_gradient(a, beta):
    n=len(a); L=2*n-1
    conv = fast_convolve(a)
    if beta <= 1e-8:
       w = np.full(L, 1.0/L)
    else:
       maxb = conv.max()
       ex = np.exp(beta*(conv - maxb))
       s = ex.sum()
       if s <= 0 or not np.isfinite(s):
           w = np.zeros(L); w[int(np.argmax(conv))]=1.0
       else: w = ex/s
    c = conv_filter(a, w[::-1])
    grad = 2.0 * c[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]
    return grad, conv, float(conv.max())
```
If beta very high, `beta*(conv-maxb)` might be -inf for many, exp 0, okay. If maxb negative? conv nonnegative mostly; okay.

`smooth_descent(a0, time_limit, lr=0.005, mom=0.9, beta_start=1.0, beta_end=3000.0, restarts=...)`:
Pseudo:
```
def smooth_descent(a0, time_limit, lr=0.005, mom=0.9, beta_start=1.0, beta_end=3000.0, noise=0.002, verbose=False):
    a=normalize_seq(a0)
    n=len(a)
    conv=fast_convolve(a); best_obj=conv.max(); best_a=a.copy()
    v=np.zeros(n); beta=beta_start; no=0; it=0; t0=time.monotonic()
    while time.monotonic()-t0 < time_limit:
       it+=1
       grad, conv, obj = smooth_gradient(a,beta)
       v = mom*v - lr*grad
       a_new = project_simplex(a+v)
       # maybe check conv_new
       new_conv = fast_convolve(a_new); new_obj=new_conv.max()
       if new_obj < best_obj - 1e-15:
          best_obj=new_obj; best_a=a_new.copy(); no=0
       else: no+=1
       a=a_new; conv=new_conv
       # schedule beta: increase gradually
       if beta < beta_end:
          beta *= math.exp(math.log(beta_end/beta_start)/max(1,time_limit/??))
       # simpler beta *= 1.02 while <= end
       # decays lr slightly every 100
       if it % 100 == 0: lr *= 0.99
       if no > 200:
          rng = np.random.default_rng(it*13)
          scale = min(0.01, noise + 0.002*no/200)
          a = project_simplex(best_a + rng.standard_normal(n)*scale)
          conv = fast_convolve(a); v=np.zeros(n); no=0; lr=0.004
    return best_a, best_obj
```
Need import math? Use np.log. Could avoid math import by `beta *= 1.01`. Simpler.
Set beta_end high 10000. lr maybe 0.002 to avoid oscillation. For n=768, gradient magnitude? b max ~1e-3, gradient maybe similar. lr 0.005 OK. But sum projection. We can use Nesterov? no.
Maybe use `lr_base=0.01 / (1+log(n))`? Set lr=0.003.
Since smooth objective scale ~1/n^2? Wait a sum=1, b coefficients ~1/n. Gradient wrt a: derivative coefficient ~ a ~1/n, weights; magnitude ~1/n (~0.001). lr 0.005 gives step 5e-6 per iteration, too small. Actually gradient of smooth max in a: ∂b/∂a ~ a values ~1/n=0.001, sum over weights maybe width n -> could be ~0.1? Let's estimate: For one active peak, gradient vector has 2a_{k-r} at n/2 positions, each ~1/n, so components ~2/n; vector L2 ~ sqrt(n)/n=1/√n. Step lr*grad ~0.005/√768 ~0.00018. Over 1000 iterations 0.18, okay. Maybe fine. To be safe lr=0.01.
But if using projected gradient, too large may oscillate. We can do multiple β.
Maybe use adaptive step: If new_obj > previous obj by more, reduce lr. Simpler:
```
step_fail=0
if new_obj > prev_obj + 1e-12: step_fail+=1; lr *=0.8; v*=0.5
else step_fail=max(0,step_fail-1); lr=min(lr*1.01, lr_max)
```

`coordinate_descent_pass` details with no lambdas. Need be efficient. Let's write candidate list generation carefully.
```
def coordinate_descent_pass(a, max_time=30.0, grid_points=14, top_k=8, max_candidates=28, recompute=True):
    a = normalize_seq(a)
    n=len(a); L=2*n-1
    conv = fast_convolve(a)
    current_obj = float(conv.max())
    best_obj = current_obj
    best_a = a.copy()
    t0=time.monotonic()
    # If n large, reduce max_candidates
    if n > 1200:
       grid_points = min(grid_points, 8); top_k=4; max_candidates=12
    for j in range(n):
       if time.monotonic()-t0 > max_time: break
       xold=a[j]
       s=1.0-xold
       if s < 1e-10:
          continue
       b = a.copy(); b[j]=0.0
       b = b / s
       D = np.zeros(L)
       st=j; en=min(j+n, L)
       if en>st: D[st:en]=b[:en-st]
       E = np.zeros(L)
       if 2*j < L: E[2*j]=1.0
       B = (conv - xold*xold*E - 2.0*xold*s*D) / (s*s)
       # Clean B: if any < -small set 0; if negative small set 0
       # Use np.maximum(B, 0.0)? Conv(b) nonnegative. This may bias but okay.
       B = np.maximum(B, 0.0)
       # But if B has small positive and D/E, for x=1 c=E; okay.
       A = E - 2.0*B
       Bc = 2.0*(D - B)
       Cc = B
       xs_list=[]
       # grid
       if grid_points > 0:
           grid = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, grid_points)
           xs_list.extend(list(grid))
       xs_list.append(xold)
       xs_list.append(0.0); xs_list.append(1.0)
       # top indices
       if top_k > 0 and len(conv)>0:
          K = min(top_k, L)
          if K == L:
             top = np.arange(L)
          else:
             top = np.argpartition(conv, -K)[-K:]
          for p in top:
             if abs(A[p]) > 1e-15:
                r = -Bc[p] / (2.0*A[p])
                if r >= -1e-12 and r <= 1.0+1e-12:
                   xs_list.append(float(np.clip(r,0.0,1.0)))
          # pairwise intersections, but limit
          added=0
          m=len(top)
          for ii in range(m):
             p=top[ii]
             for jj in range(ii+1,m):
                q=top[jj]
                da=A[p]-A[q]; db=Bc[p]-Bc[q]; dc=Cc[p]-Cc[q]
                if abs(da) > 1e-14:
                   disc=db*db - 4.0*da*dc
                   if disc >= -1e-14:
                      if disc < 0: disc=0.0
                      sqrt_disc=np.sqrt(disc)
                      r1=(-db - sqrt_disc)/(2.0*da)
                      r2=(-db + sqrt_disc)/(2.0*da)
                      for r in (r1,r2):
                         if r >= -1e-12 and r <= 1.0+1e-12:
                            if len(xs_list) < 200: xs_list.append(float(np.clip(r,0.0,1.0)))
                            added+=1
                elif abs(db) > 1e-14:
                   r = -dc/db
                   if r >= -1e-12 and r <= 1.0+1e-12:
                      if len(xs_list) < 200: xs_list.append(float(np.clip(r,0.0,1.0)))
       # unique and cap
       xs_arr = np.array(xs_list, dtype=np.float64)
       xs_arr = xs_arr[(xs_arr>=0.0)&(xs_arr<=1.0)]
       if xs_arr.size ==0:
          continue
       xs_arr = np.sort(xs_arr)
       # manual unique
       uniq=[xs_arr[0]]
       for val in xs_arr[1:]:
          if val - uniq[-1] > 1e-8:
             uniq.append(val)
       xs_arr=np.array(uniq)
       if xs_arr.size > max_candidates:
          # prioritize grid? Simpler: take a mix: first max_candidates sorted (skews low), bad.
          # Use selection: always include 0,1,xold and then evenly from rest.
          # We can sort by distance to xold? But far minima possible.
          # Maybe include 0,1,xold, and linspace 0..1 12, plus closest roots.
          base = np.linspace(0,1, min(16,max_candidates))
          candidates = np.array([0.0,1.0,xold])
          rest = xs_arr
          # add those not in base? To keep simple, take np.linspace if too many.
          # But intersections may be important. Use: if size > max, take every k-th from sorted plus endpoints.
          idx = np.linspace(0, xs_arr.size-1, max_candidates).astype(int)
          xs_arr = xs_arr[idx]
          # ensure endpoints and xold
          xs_arr = np.concatenate((xs_arr, np.array([0.0,1.0,xold])))
          xs_arr = np.unique(np.clip(xs_arr,0,1))
          if xs_arr.size > max_candidates:
              xs_arr = xs_arr[:max_candidates]
       # evaluate in chunks to limit memory
       best_val = current_obj
       best_x = xold
       chunk_size = max(1, 200000 // L) # about 200k elements per chunk
       for start in range(0, xs_arr.size, chunk_size):
           chunk = xs_arr[start:start+chunk_size]
           ox = 1.0 - chunk
           vals = (ox*ox)[:,None]*B + (2.0*chunk*ox)[:,None]*D + (chunk*chunk)[:,None]*E
           m = vals.max(axis=1)
           idx_local = int(np.argmin(m))
           if m[idx_local] < best_val:
              best_val = float(m[idx_local]); best_x = float(chunk[idx_local])
       # optional refine around best_x with local grid if best_val improved
       if best_val < current_obj - 1e-12 and 0.0 < best_x < 1.0:
          refine = np.linspace(max(0.0,best_x-0.05), min(1.0,best_x+0.05), 16)
          # include best_x
          refine = np.concatenate((refine, np.array([best_x])))
          ox=1.0-refine
          vals=(ox*ox)[:,None]*B + (2.0*refine*ox)[:,None]*D + (refine*refine)[:,None]*E
          m=vals.max(axis=1); idx=int(np.argmin(m))
          if m[idx] < best_val:
             best_val=float(m[idx]); best_x=float(refine[idx])
       if best_val < current_obj - 1e-13:
           a_new = a.copy()
           if best_x <= 1e-15:
              a_new[j]=0.0
           elif best_x >= 1.0-1e-15:
              a_new[:] = 0.0; a_new[j]=1.0
           else:
              a_new[j]=best_x
              mask = np.ones(n, dtype=bool); mask[j]=False
              a_new[mask] *= (1.0 - best_x) / s
              a_new = np.maximum(a_new, 0.0)
              ss = a_new.sum()
              if ss > 1e-15: a_new = a_new/ss
           conv_new = (1.0-best_x)**2 * B + (2.0*best_x*(1.0-best_x))*D + (best_x*best_x)*E
           conv_new = np.maximum(conv_new, 0.0)
           new_obj = float(conv_new.max())
           if new_obj < best_obj - 1e-14:
              best_obj = new_obj; best_a = a_new.copy()
           a = a_new
           conv = conv_new
           current_obj = new_obj
    if recompute:
       a = normalize_seq(best_a if best_obj < current_obj else a)
       # Use best_a if improved else a; but if a has improvements not best? best is min.
       conv = fast_convolve(a)
       obj = float(conv.max())
       return a, obj
    return a, current_obj
```
Potential memory: `vals` chunk. Good.
But `B` clipping to 0 may distort candidate at x=1: c=E, ok. If B derived has negative large due to conv numeric, clipping helps. However if B should be zero at some k but D/E make c negative for some x, clipping B might c still negative? We clip final conv_new to0. Objective max uses positive. Good.
One issue: For x=1, formula `(1-x)^2B+...+1*E` okay. But if best_x=1, others zero, conv has single 1 at 2j, max 1, bad; won't choose unless n=1.

`transfer_polish` similar but can use current conv. Need no nested.
```
def transfer_polish(a, max_time=30.0, grid_points=18, rng_seed=12345, verbose=False):
    a=normalize_seq(a); n=len(a); L=2*n-1
    conv=fast_convolve(a); current_obj=float(conv.max()); best_obj=current_obj; best_a=a.copy()
    t0=time.monotonic(); rng=np.random.Default_rng? Actually np.random.default_rng
    trials=0
    while time.monotonic()-t0 < max_time:
       trials+=1
       i=int(rng.integers(0,n)); j=int(rng.integers(0,n))
       if i==j: continue
       ai=a[i]; aj=a[j]
       if ai + aj < 1e-15: continue
       C=np.zeros(L)
       C[i:i+n]=a
       C[j:j+n]-=a
       E=np.zeros(L)
       if 2*i<L: E[2*i]+=1.0
       if i+j<L: E[i+j]-=2.0
       if 2*j<L: E[2*j]+=1.0
       lo=-ai; hi=aj
       if hi <= lo + 1e-15: continue
       xs=np.linspace(lo,hi,grid_points)
       best_x=0.0; best_val=current_obj
       chunk_size=max(1, 200000//L)
       for start...:
          chunk=xs[start:...
          vals=conv + (2.0*chunk)[:,None]*C + (chunk*chunk)[:,None]*E
          m=vals.max(axis=1)
          idx=argmin; if m[idx]<best_val...
       if best_val < current_obj - 1e-13 and best_x !=0:
          a_new=a.copy(); a_new[i]+=best_x; a_new[j]-=best_x
          a_new=np.maximum(a_new,0); ss=a_new.sum(); if ss>0: a_new/=ss
          conv_new=conv + 2*best_x*C + best_x*best_x*E
          conv_new=np.maximum(conv_new,0)
          new_obj=float(conv_new.max())
          if new_obj < best_obj - 1e-14: best_obj=new_obj; best_a=a_new.copy()
          a=a_new; conv=conv_new; current_obj=new_obj
       # optional if long no improve try targeted? Maybe track no_imp and add noise.
    a=normalize_seq(best_a); conv=fast_convolve(a); obj=float(conv.max()); return a,obj
```
But random transfer with `best_x=0` may fail if grid only includes 0 and no improvement direction. Good.
Could also include direction reverse? Since i,j random, both directions over time. But for each pair only one direction (i inc, j dec). Random will sample reverse sometimes. Fine.

def suggestion_pairs(a, conv, max_pairs=100):
    n=len(a); pairs=[]
    K=min(30, 2*n-1)
    top=np.argpartition(conv,-K)[-K:]
    for k in top:
       js_start=max(0,k-n+1); js_end=min(n-1,k)
       if js_end-js_start<=0: continue

       if p!=q: pairs.append((int(p),int(q))) # transfer from q to p => e_p - e_q
    # unique
    return pairs

`lp_polish(a, time_limit)`:
```
def lp_polish(a, max_time=120.0):
    a=normalize_seq(a); n=len(a)
    if n > 1200: # avoid big
       return a, fast_convolve(a).max()
    t0=time.monotonic()
    current_obj=fast_convolve(a).max(); best_a=a.copy(); best_obj=current_obj
    while time...:
       conv=fast_convolve(a); M=float(conv.max())
       g=lp_direction(a, M)
       if g is None: break
       gs=g.sum()
       if gs <=1e-12: break
       g=g/gs
       # try updates
       best_t=0; best_obj_local=best_obj
       for t in [0.05,0.1,0.2,0.3,0.5,0.7,0.9]:
          h=(1-t)*a + t*g
          h=np.maximum(h,0); hs=h.sum(); if hs<=0: continue; h/=hs
          obj=fast_convolve(h).max()
          if obj < best_obj_local -1e-14:
             best_obj_local=obj; best_t=t
       if best_t>0:
          h=(1-best_t)*a + best_t*g; ...; a=h; best_a=h; best_obj=best_obj_local
       else:
          # add small noise and continue maybe
          if time.monotonic()-t0 > max_time*0.5: break
          rng=np.random.default_rng(int(time.monotonic()*1000)%100000)
          a=project_simplex(a + rng.standard_normal(n)*0.002)
    return normalize_seq(best_a), fast_convolve(normalize_seq(best_a)).max()
```
But `best_obj` variable in loop: We should keep global best, not local to current a? For direction, if no improvement from current a, maybe from a different noise. Simpler: update a if any t improves current obj; accept even if not global? It will improve. Track best_a global.
`lp_direction` build A dense. Use `scipy.optimize.linprog`. Need import at top:
```
try:
    from scipy.optimize import linprog
    SCIPY_OK=True
except Exception:
    SCIPY_OK=False
```
Function:
```
def lp_direction(f, M):
    if not SCIPY_OK: return None
    n=len(f); L=2*n-1
    # build A dense maybe
    k=np.arange(L)[:,None]
    i=np.arange(n)[None,:]
    idx=k-i
    mask=(idx>=0)&(idx<n)
    A=np.zeros((L,n), dtype=np.float64)
    A[mask]=f[idx[mask]]
    c=-np.ones(n)
    b=np.full(L, M)
    try:
       res=linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=(0.0,None), method='highs', options={'presolve': True})
    except Exception:
       return None
    if res.success and res.x is not None:
       x=res.x
       if np.isfinite(x).all():
          return np.maximum(x,0)
    return None
```
Potential memory/time. For n=768, dense 1.2M; okay. For n=1024, 4.2M 33MB; okay. Linprog may be slow. Set max_time per solve? We can't easily interrupt. Limit calls to maybe 20. Use only if n<=900? Parent 768. To be safe n<=1000.
Could use sparse to reduce memory and maybe faster. HiGHS supports sparse. Build sparse with `scipy.sparse` if available. But dense may be faster for small. We can use dense.

Need avoid too many LP solves. In `lp_polish`, each solve could be seconds. Use max_solves parameter and time check. If a solve takes too long, not detected. We can set `max_solves=15` and only call when time remaining > 30. Maybe use `method='highs'` presolve. It should be okay.

But if scipy not installed, lp_polish returns quickly.

`combined_polish(a0, time_limit, do_coord=True, do_transfer=True, do_smooth=True, do_lp=False, verbose=False)`:
This function top-level, uses loops no nested. We'll define phases within.
Pseudo:
```
def combined_polish(a0, time_limit, verbose=False):
    a=normalize_seq(a0)
    best_a=a.copy()
    best_obj=fast_convolve(a).max()
    t0=time.monotonic()
    phase=0
    n=len(a)
    use_coord = n <= 1200
    use_lp = n <= 900 and SCIPY_OK
    while time.monotonic()-t0 < time_limit:
       rem=time_limit-(time.monotonic()-t0)
       if rem < 10: break
       if phase % 6 == 0:
          t_alloc=min(30,rem)
          a_tmp,obj=smooth_descent(best_a, t_alloc, lr=0.004, beta_start=1.0, beta_end=500.0)
          if obj < best_obj: best_a, best_obj=a_tmp,obj
       elif phase %6 ==1 and use_coord:
          t_alloc=min(60,rem)
          a_tmp,obj=coordinate_descent_pass(best_a, t_alloc)
          if obj < best_obj: ...
       elif phase %6 ==2:
          t_alloc=min(45,rem)
          a_tmp,obj=transfer_polish(best_a,t_alloc)
          if obj < best_obj: ...
       elif phase %6 ==3:
          t_alloc=min(30,rem)
          a_tmp,obj=smooth_descent(best_a,t_alloc,lr=0.002,beta_start=50,beta_end=5000.0)
          ...
       elif phase %6 ==4:
          if use_lp and rem>60:
             a_tmp,obj=lp_polish(best_a, min(60,rem))
             ...
          else:
             a_tmp,obj=transfer_polish(best_a,min(30,rem),rng_seed=...)
       else:
          # coordinate again or small smooth
       phase+=1
    return normalize_seq(best_a), best_obj
```

- conv=conv_new

`smooth_descent` adaptation:
```
def smooth_descent(a0, time_limit, lr=0.005, mom=0.85, beta_start=1.0, beta_end=3000.0, seed=123):
   a=normalize_seq(a0)
   conv=fast_convolve(a)
   prev_obj=float(conv.max()); best_obj=prev_obj; best_a=a.copy()
   v=np.zeros(n); beta=float(beta_start); t0=...
   it=0; no=0; lr_max=lr*2; 
   while ...:
      it+=1
      grad=smooth_gradient_from_conv(a, conv, beta)
      # normalize gradient to have average? projected handles.
      v = mom*v - lr*grad
      a_new=project_simplex(a+v)
      conv_new=fast_convolve(a_new)
      obj_new=float(conv_new.max())
      if obj_new < best_obj -1e-15: update
      if obj_new > prev_obj + 5e-5 * max(1,prev_obj)? maybe adapt lr. But objective may not decrease. Use if obj_new > prev_obj + 1e-10: lr*=0.8; v*=0.5; no+=2 else lr=min(lr_max,lr*1.02); if improved no=0 else no+=1
      prev_obj=obj_new; a=a_new; conv=conv_new
      beta *= 1.02; if beta>beta_end: beta=beta_end
      if no > 250: noise
   return best_a,best_obj
```
The objective `max conv` not smooth; it can increase while smooth decreases. Adaptation maybe too aggressive. Could just use fixed small lr and no adapt. Use no_improve noise.
Set lr based on n: maybe `lr = 0.02 / np.sqrt(n)`. For n=768 ~0.00072, perhaps too small? Let's estimate gradient magnitude. For one active peak, grad components ~2a ~0.0026, L2~0.0001? Step 0.0007*0.0001? Wait grad vector components not summed; projection step is vector. If lr=0.001, step components ~1e-6, too small? Need think: a_i ~1/n=0.0013. Derivative wrt a_i of b_k is 2a_{k-i} ~0.0026. Gradient vector has many components up to 0.0026, L2 maybe sqrt(n)*0.0026 ~0.07. lr=0.01 gives step L2 0.0007, per iter coordinate change ~1e-6? Actually L2 0.0007 over 768 coords => typical 2.5e-5. Over 10000 =>0.25, enough. So lr 0.01 good. Use lr=0.005.
Momentum 0.9.

We can also use projection onto simplex with `v` that may have positive sum; projection accounts. Good.

Potential problem: Smooth max gradient with β low gives average direction; might move toward uniform (bad) if β too low. We can start β maybe 20 to focus on max peaks. Use β_start=5, then increase. For global optimization, low β might reduce average but exact max. Maybe start 5.

`beta_end=10000`. Increase factor 1.05 per iter to reach in 100 iters. But if high too soon, local. We'll do multiple calls with different β ranges: early β 2-100, later 100-10000.

`coordinate_descent_pass` may be the main improver. Need ensure it doesn't spend too much time if n small. We can call multiple passes. It recomputes conv at end. It updates `conv` incrementally; if many updates, B derivation from approximate conv may drift; every pass recompute. Good.

`project_simplex` implementation: Need robust for n large. Standard:
```
def project_simplex(v):
    n=len(v)
    if n==1: return np.array([max(v[0],0)])
    u=np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cssv=np.cumsum(u)
    cond = u * np.arange(1,n+1) > (cssv - 1)
    if not cond.any(): return np.ones(n)/n
    rho = np.nonzero(cond)[0][-1]
    theta = (cssv[rho] - 1.0) / (rho + 1.0)
    return np.maximum(v - theta, 0.0)
```
Works for v finite. Ensure v no NaN; if noise huge? okay.

`project_complement`:
```
def project_complement(v):
    n=len(v)
    a=np.empty(n)
    half=n//2
    for i in range(half):
       j=n-1-i
       s=n*(v[i]-v[j])
       if s >1: s=1
       elif s<-1: s=-1
       a[i]=(1+s)/n; a[j]=(1-s)/n
    if n%2: a[half]=1.0/n
    return a
```
This output sum 1 exactly? Due float maybe; normalize.

def make_sine_seed(n, mode=1, alpha=0.8, phase=0.0):
    i=np.arange(n)
    u=np.sin(2*np.pi*mode*i/n + phase)
    # enforce antisym? If phase nonzero, not anti. For anti, phase=0 and any mode. For non-sym, maybe use cos? We'll allow.
    amax=np.max(np.abs(u)); if amax>0: u=u/amax
    a=(1+alpha*u)/n
    return normalize_seq(a)
```

`make_random_antisy_seed(n, num_modes=8, rng_seed=123, alpha=0.8, lowpass=0.2)`:
Generate random coefficients for first `num_modes` sine modes? Or random array anti-sym low-pass. Simpler:
```
def make_random_antisy_seed(n, num_modes=8, alpha=0.8, seed=123):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    i=np.arange(n)
    u=np.zeros(n)
    for m in range(1,num_modes+1):
       coeff=rng.normal(0,1.0/np.sqrt(m))
       u += coeff*np.sin(2*np.pi*m*i/n)
    amax=np.max(np.abs(u))
    if amax<=0: u=np.zeros(n)
    else: u/=amax
    a=(1+alpha*u)/n
    return normalize_seq(a)
```
May not be anti if phase? sine with integer m anti. Good.
Could also add uniform noise low-pass: `u = rng.normal; u -= u[::-1]; lowpass by FFT zero high freq; normalize`. But sine modes enough.

`interpolate_to_length` as previous.

`consider` without closure: We'll in propose_candidate maintain `best_a`, `best_obj_internal`, `best_score`. For each candidate, call a top-level function `evaluate_candidate_internal(a, use_direct=False)` returns (a_norm, obj). But to update final score, call evaluate_sequence. We can write:
```
def measure_sequence(a):
    a=normalize_seq(a)
    if len(a)>2500:
       obj=float(fast_convolve(a).max())*2*len(a)
    else:
       obj=float(np.convolve(a,a).max())*2*len(a)
    return obj
```
But direct np.convolve for n=2500 maybe 6.25M, okay. Use direct for scoring best. For internal optimization, use fast maxconv.

Maybe hidden evaluate_sequence direct convolve; actual score may differ from 2n maxconv if sum not 1? We normalize, so same. If our `abs` internal uses direct max, good.

In local search, we use conv.max not score; comparing same n. But if n changes, internal best comparing maxconv not enough; need score=2n maxconv. We need `consider` compare actual score estimate `2n*maxconv`. In `combined_polish`, it optimizes `maxconv` for fixed n. But if different lengths, a lower maxconv with larger n may have worse score. So top-level must compare `2n*obj`. Within fixed seed n, minimizing obj equivalent. For interpolation, length changes; compare score.

In `combined_polish` for fixed n, return best obj (maxconv). Top-level score =2n*obj.

When considering candidate of length m, compute `score_est=2*m*obj`. Keep best if score_est < best_score_est - tol. But evaluate_sequence may give exact. Use `direct_score(a)`.

`direct_score(a)`:
```
def direct_score(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=float)
    a=np.maximum(a,0)
    s=a.sum()
    if s <0.01: return inf
    a=a/s
    m=len(a)
    if m <= 2048:
       c=np.convolve(a,a)
    else:
       c=fast_convolve(a)
    return float(2*m*c.max())
```
Use for candidate updates, not too often.

Let's refine `combined_polish` to be efficient and use current best internally. It should accept `a0`, `time_limit`, `verbose`, `seed`.
```
def combined_polish(a0, time_limit, verbose=False, seed=123):
    a=normalize_seq(a0)
    n=len(a)
    best_a=a.copy()
    conv=fast_convolve(a); best_obj=float(conv.max())
    t0=time.monotonic()
    phase=0
    no_improve=0
    use_coord = n <= 1100
    use_lp = (n <= 850) and SCIPY_OK
    rng_seed = seed
    while time.monotonic() - t0 < time_limit:
       rem = time_limit - (time.monotonic()-t0)
       if rem < 8: break
       start_obj=best_obj
       if phase % 8 == 0:
          al=min(25.0, rem)
          a_tmp, obj=smooth_descent(best_a, al, lr=0.006, mom=0.88, beta_start=2.0, beta_end=200.0, seed=rng_seed+phase*101)
       elif phase %8 ==1 and use_coord:
          al=min(50.0, rem)
          a_tmp,obj=coordinate_descent_pass(best_a, al, grid_points=14, top_k=8, max_candidates=28)
       elif phase %8 ==2:
          al=min(40.0, rem)
          a_tmp,obj=transfer_polish(best_a, al, grid_points=18, rng_seed=rng_seed+phase*101+1)
       elif phase %8 ==3:
          al=min(25.0, rem)
          a_tmp,obj=smooth_descent(best_a, al, lr=0.003, mom=0.92, beta_start=50.0, beta_end=3000.0, seed=rng_seed+phase*101+2)
       elif phase %8 ==4:
          if use_lp and rem > 80:
             al=min(50.0, rem)
             a_tmp,obj=lp_polish(best_a, al, seed=rng_seed+phase*101)
          else:
             al=min(30.0, rem)
             a_tmp,obj=transfer_polish(best_a, al, grid_points=12, rng_seed=rng_seed+phase*101+3)
       elif phase %8 ==5:
          al=min(30.0, rem)
          a_tmp,obj=coordinate_descent_pass(best_a, al, grid_points=10, top_k=6, max_candidates=20) if use_coord else transfer_polish(best_a, al, grid_points=12, rng_seed=...)
       elif phase %8 ==6:
          # noise + smooth
          rng=np.random.default_rng(rng_seed+phase*101+4)
          scale=0.003 * max(0.3, 1.0 - 0.02*no_improve)
          a_noise=project_simplex(best_a + rng.standard_normal(n)*scale)
          al=min(30.0, rem)
          a_tmp,obj=smooth_descent(a_noise, al, lr=0.004, mom=0.9, beta_start=10.0, beta_end=1000.0, seed=...)
       else:
          al=min(20.0, rem)
          a_tmp,obj=transfer_polish(best_a, al, grid_points=16, rng_seed=...)
       if obj < best_obj - 1e-14:
          best_obj=obj; best_a=normalize_seq(a_tmp); no_improve=0
          if verbose: print(...)
       else:
          no_improve += 1
       phase += 1
    a=normalize_seq(best_a)
    conv=fast_convolve(a); obj=float(conv.max())
    return a, obj
```
Need if phase uses coordinate but use_coord false, fallback. We can simplify with if/else without modulo complex? This is okay. But repeated calls to `coordinate_descent_pass` can be expensive. Time allocations sum per phase. Phase loop will continue until time. Good.

Need `suggested_pairs` top-level. It uses `conv` and `a`. It should return list of (p,q) where p low, q high for each active lag, meaning transfer from q to p. In transfer direction e_i - e_j, i inc (p), j dec (q). Good.
```
def suggested_pairs(a, conv, max_pairs=80):
   n=len(a); L=len(conv)
   K=min(20,L)
   top=np.argpartition(conv,-K)[-K:]
   seen=set(); pairs=[]
   for k in top:
      s=max(0,k-n+1); e=min(n-1,k)
      if e<=s: continue? if e>=s
      idx=np.arange(s,e+1)
      vals=a[idx]
      # To avoid always same, take min and max; also second max
      p_local=int(np.argmin(vals)); q_local=int(np.argmax(vals))
      p=int(idx[p_local]); q=int(idx[q_local])
      if p!=q and (p,q) not in seen:
         seen.add((p,q)); pairs.append((p,q))
      if len(pairs)>=max_pairs: break
   # add high-to-low overall? maybe top 20 values
   order=np.argsort(a)[::-1]
   for q in order[:20]:
      # choose p min a not q
      p=int(np.argmin(a))
      if p==q: p=int(np.argmin(np.where(a==np.min(a), np.inf?)))? Simpler skip
      if p!=q:
         if (p,q) not in seen and len(pairs)<max_pairs: add
   return pairs
```

Function:
```
def make_alpha_tail_seed(n, alpha=0.5):
    a=np.zeros(n); a[0]=1.0
    for i in range(n-1): a[i+1]=a[i]*(i+alpha)/(i+1)
    return normalize(a)
```

`make_alpha_tail_seed(n, alpha)` details:

```
def make_alpha_tail_seed(n, alpha=0.5):
    a=np.empty(n); a[0]=1.0
    for i in range(1,n):
       a[i]=a[i-1]*((i-1)+alpha)/i
    a=np.maximum(a,0)
    s=a.sum(); if s<=0: a=np.ones(n)/n else a/=s
    return a
```
For alpha=1, a_i=1. For alpha=0.5, decreases. If alpha negative? avoid.
Could overflow for alpha large? alpha<=1, terms <=1, no.

Let's refine `smooth_descent` with gradient from conv to reduce FFT.
```
def smooth_gradient_from_conv(a, conv, beta):
    n=len(a); L=2*n-1
    if beta <= 1e-6:
       w = np.full(L, 1.0/L)
    else:
       maxb=float(conv.max())
       ex=np.exp(beta*(conv - maxb))
       s=ex.sum()
       if not np.isfinite(s) or s<=0:
          w=np.zeros(L); w[int(np.argmax(conv))]=1.0
       else: w=ex/s
    c=conv_filter(a, w[::-1])
    grad=2.0 * c[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]
    # maybe subtract mean to reduce projection work? Projected gradient can handle; subtracting mean preserves tangent. We can do grad -= grad.mean() for efficiency.
    grad -= np.mean(grad)
    return grad
```
Subtracting mean is okay because sum constraint; projection after adding v maybe less shift. Good.

`conv_filter` uses FFT size; if one array length 1? okay.

`smooth_descent` adaptation detail:
```
def smooth_descent(a0, time_limit, lr=0.005, mom=0.88, beta_start=2.0, beta_end=2000.0, seed=123, verbose=False):
    a=normalize_seq(a0); n=len(a)
    conv=fast_convolve(a); prev_obj=float(conv.max()); best_obj=prev_obj; best_a=a.copy()
    v=np.zeros(n); beta=float(beta_start); t0=time.monotonic(); it=0; no=0; rng=np.random.default_rng(seed); lr_max=lr*3
    while time.monotonic()-t0 < time_limit:
       it+=1
       grad=smooth_gradient_from_conv(a,conv,beta)
       v = mom*v - lr*grad
       a_new=project_simplex(a+v)
       conv_new=fast_convolve(a_new)
       obj_new=float(conv_new.max())
       if obj_new < best_obj - 1e-15:
          best_obj=obj_new; best_a=a_new.copy(); no=0
       else: no+=1
       # adapt lr if objective increased a lot
       if obj_new > prev_obj:
          lr = max(1e-5, lr*0.85)
          v *= 0.5
       else:
          lr = min(lr_max, lr*1.02)
       prev_obj=obj_new; a=a_new; conv=conv_new
       if beta < beta_end:
          beta *= 1.03
          if beta > beta_end: beta=beta_end
       if no > 300:
          scale = 0.003 + 0.002*min(3, no//300)
          a = project_simplex(best_a + rng.standard_normal(n)*scale)
          conv = fast_convolve(a)
          v=np.zeros(n)
          no=0
          lr=0.004
       if verbose and it % 1000 ==0: print(...)
    return normalize_seq(best_a), float(fast_convolve(best_a).max())
```
If beta increases, objective prev_obj not comparable? It is exact max, yes.
Momentum maybe 0.9. Good.

`transfer_polish` with suggested pairs:
Need ground time. Implementation details:
```
def transfer_polish(a0, max_time=30.0, grid_points=18, rng_seed=123, verbose=False):
    a=normalize_seq(a0); n=len(a); L=2*n-1
    conv=fast_convolve(a); current_obj=float(conv.max()); best_obj=current_obj; best_a=a.copy()
    t0=time.monotonic(); rng=np.random.default_rng(rng_seed); no=0; trial=0
    pairs=suggested_pairs(a,conv,100)
    pidx=0
    while time.monotonic()-t0 < max_time:
       trial+=1
       if pidx < len(pairs):
          i,j=pairs[pidx]; pidx+=1
       else:
          if trial % 1000 == 0:
             pairs=suggested_pairs(a,conv,60); pidx=0
          i=int(rng.integers(0,n)); j=int(rng.integers(0,n))
          if i==j: continue
       ai=a[i]; aj=a[j]
       if ai + aj < 1e-15: continue
       C=np.zeros(L)
       C[i:i+n]=a
       C[j:j+n]-=a
       E=np.zeros(L)
       if 2*i<L: E[2*i]+=1.0
       s_ij=i+j
       if s_ij<L: E[s_ij]-=2.0
       if 2*j<L: E[2*j]+=1.0
       lo=-ai; hi=aj
       if hi <= lo + 1e-15: continue
       xs=np.linspace(lo,hi,grid_points)
       best_x=0.0; best_val=current_obj
       chunk_size=max(1, 200000//max(1,L))
       for start in range(0,len(xs),chunk_size):
           chunk=xs[start:start+chunk_size]
           vals = conv + (2.0*chunk)[:,None]*C + (chunk*chunk)[:,None]*E
           m=vals.max(axis=1)
           idx=int(np.argmin(m))
           if m[idx] < best_val:
              best_val=float(m[idx]); best_x=float(chunk[idx])
       if best_val < current_obj - 1e-13 and abs(best_x) > 1e-15:
           a_new=a.copy()
           a_new[i]+=best_x; a_new[j]-=best_x
           a_new=np.maximum(a_new,0.0)
           ss=a_new.sum()
           if ss <=1e-15: continue
           a_new /= ss
           conv_new = conv + 2.0*best_x*C + best_x*best_x*E
           conv_new=np.maximum(conv_new,0.0)
           new_obj=float(conv_new.max())
           if new_obj < best_obj - 1e-14:
              best_obj=new_obj; best_a=a_new.copy()
           a=a_new; conv=conv_new; current_obj=new_obj; no=0
       else:
           no += 1
       if no > 3000:
           # small global noise to escape
           scale=0.002
           a=project_simplex(best_a + rng.standard_normal(n)*scale)
           conv=fast_convolve(a); current_obj=float(conv.max())
           pairs=suggested_pairs(a,conv,60); pidx=0; no=0
    a=normalize_seq(best_a); conv=fast_convolve(a); obj=float(conv.max())
    return a,obj
```
If `best_x` endpoint making a_j negative due to rounding, clip and renormalize. The conv_new formula assumes exact transfer; after clip/normalize if slight, error tiny. We can do: if new_obj improvement, set `a=a_new; conv=fast_convolve(a); current_obj=conv.max()`? But new_obj from formula may be slightly optimistic. To avoid drift, after accepting, recompute conv via fast_convolve. It costs one FFT. Good.

a=a_new; conv=fast_convolve(a); current_obj=float(conv.max())

```

`suggested_pairs` with set:
```
def suggested_pairs(a, conv, max_pairs=80):
    n=len(a); L=len(conv)
    if L <=0: return []
    K=min(30, L)
    top=np.argpartition(conv, -K)[-K:]
    seen=set(); pairs=[]
    for k in top:
       s=max(0, int(k)-n+1); e=min(n-1, int(k))
       if e < s: continue
       idx=np.arange(s,e+1)
       vals=a[idx]
       ploc=int(np.argmin(vals)); qloc=int(np.argmax(vals))
       p=int(idx[ploc]); q=int(idx[qloc])
       if p!=q:
          key=(p,q)
          if key not in seen:
             seen.add(key); pairs.append(key)
             if len(pairs)>=max_pairs: break
    # add global high-to-low
    if len(pairs) < max_pairs:
       order=np.argsort(a)
       lows=order[:max(1,n//10)]
       highs=order[::-1][:max(1,n//10)]
       for q in highs:
          for p in lows:
             p=int(p); q=int(q)
             if p!=q and (p,q) not in seen:
                seen.add((p,q)); pairs.append((p,q))
                if len(pairs)>=max_pairs: break
          if len(pairs)>=max_pairs: break
    return pairs
```
It uses `set` of tuples; okay. No lambdas.

```
def lp_direction(f, M):
    if not SCIPY_OPT_OK: return None
    n=len(f)
    if n > 850: return None
    L=2*n-1
    k=np.arange(L)[:,None]; i=np.arange(n)[None,:]
    idx=k-i
    mask=(idx>=0)&(idx<n)
    A=np.zeros((L,n),dtype=np.float64)
    A[mask]=f[idx[mask]]
    c=-np.ones(n)
    b=np.full(L,M,dtype=np.float64)
    try:
       res=linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=(0.0, None), method='highs',
                   options={'presolve': True, 'dual_feasibility_tolerance': 1e-9})
    except Exception:
       try:
          res=linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=(0.0, None), method='simplex')
       except Exception:
          return None
    if res.success and res.x is not None:
       x=np.asarray(res.x,dtype=np.float64)
       if np.isfinite(x).all() and x.sum()>1e-12:
          return np.maximum(x,0)
    return None
```
`bounds=(0.0,None)` accepted? In scipy linprog, bounds can be tuple (lb, ub) applied to all? I think yes. To be safe use list of tuples? That would be large. `bounds=(0.0, None)` should work for new scipy. If not, fallback maybe error. Good.
Memory: `A[mask]=f[idx[mask]]` with `idx[mask]` int array size ~n^2/2? For n=850, ~360k, okay. Actually dense L*n ~1.4M. Good.
For n=768, nonzeros ~295k? Wait matrix convolution full has about n^2? For each k row length up to n, total ≈n^2 (not half) because for k 0..2n-2 row lengths 1..n..1 sum = n^2. Nonzeros n^2. For 768, 590k. Dense 1.2M. Good.

```
def lp_polish(a0, max_time=60.0, seed=123, max_solves=5):
    a=normalize_seq(a0); n=len(a)
    if n > 850: return a, fast_convolve(a).max()
    conv=fast_convolve(a); best_obj=float(conv.max()); best_a=a.copy()
    t0=time.monotonic(); rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    solves=0; stall=0
    while time.monotonic()-t0 < max_time and solves < max_solves:
       rem=max_time-(time.monotonic()-t0)
       if rem < 20: break
       conv=fast_convolve(a); M=float(conv.max())
       if M <= 1e-15: break
       g=lp_direction(a, M)
       solves +=1
       if g is None: break
       gs=float(g.sum())
       if gs <=1e-12: break
       g=g/gs
       old_a=a.copy()
       improved=False
       for t in (0.05,0.1,0.2,0.3,0.5,0.7,0.9):
          h=(1.0-t)*a + t*g
          h=np.maximum(h,0.0); hs=h.sum()
          if hs <=1e-12: continue
          h=h/hs
          obj=float(fast_convolve(h).max())
          if obj < best_obj - 1e-14:
             best_obj=obj; best_a=h.copy(); a=h; improved=True; break
       if improved:
          stall=0
       else:
          stall+=1
          if stall >= 2:
             a=project_simplex(best_a + rng.standard_normal(n)*0.003)
             stall=0
             if solves >= max_solves: break
    a=normalize_seq(best_a); obj=float(fast_convolve(a).max())
    return a,obj
```

Now, let's verify gradient formula with `grad -= mean(grad)`: Does subtracting mean preserve projected gradient? Since any vector differing by constant has same projection onto simplex? Projection of a+v for v and v - c*1: a+v-c1 projected? The feasible set sum=1, projection of shifted vector may change? The Euclidean projection onto simplex is not invariant to adding constants to v? Wait projection of a + v where v -> v - c*1 gives a+v - c*1. The projection onto simplex of y and y-c1: Are they same? For a point on simplex, the projection of y-c1 maybe projection(y) - c1? Because shifting y by constant shifts the optimal theta by c? Yes projection_simplex(y - c1) = projection_simplex(y) - c1? The sort and theta add c; max(v-theta) = max(v - theta - c) = projection - c? But then we are projecting a + new_v? Wait a is on simplex. We consider candidate z = a + v. If v -> v - c1, z -> z - c1. Projection onto simplex of z-c1 is projection(z) - c1? Since if p=proj(z) satisfies sum p=1, then p-c1 has sum 1-n c not on simplex. Not that. Actually projection of z-c1 is not simple. Adding constant to gradient before projection is not guaranteed equivalent. However in projected gradient methods on simplex, one often subtracts the mean of gradient to keep zero sum move, but projection of a - lr(g-mean) vs a - lr g may differ because projection handles sum. Is subtracting mean safe? The tangent to simplex is zero-sum; the steepest descent direction in Euclidean metric constrained to sum is the projection of -g onto tangent, which is - (g - mean(g)). Then line search would maintain sum exactly without bounding? Projection of a + step*tangent = a + step*tangent because sum unchanged, but may violate nonneg; projection onto simplex of a + step*tangent equals a+step*tangent if it is already sum1 and nonneg? If not nonneg, projecting a+step*tangent (sum1) onto simplex with nonneg is just clipping? The Euclidean projection of a point with sum1 onto simplex is itself if nonneg; if negative, projection fixes. So using tangent direction is valid and often better. The projection of a + (v - c1) is equivalent to projecting along tangent; not same as a+v but since v had arbitrary mean, the optimal constrained step should use tangent part only. Mean part just shifts the point off simplex, which projection would undo partly. So subtracting mean is good.
But our `v` momentum includes gradient mean subtracted each step, so v has mean near0. Good.

In coordinate/transfer, we preserve sum exactly.

`fast_convolve`:
Use `np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()`. Ensure finite? Could replace nan with0.
```
def fast_convolve(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
    if n <= 96:
       return np.convolve(a,a)
    size=2*n-1
    fft_size=1 << (size-1).bit_length()
    A=np.fft.rfft(a, fft_size)
    c=np.fft.irfft(A*A, fft_size)[:size]
    return c
```
We clip a to nonnegative because optimization assumes. For gradient, a should be nonneg. Clipping inside conv may hide tiny negative, okay.

`conv_filter` similar but do not clip b? For gradient, w nonnegative. We can clip a nonnegative, b nonnegative. But if b has tiny negative from softmax? no. Use:
```
def conv_filter(a,b):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel(); b=np.asarray(b,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    na, nb = a.size,b.size
    if na==0 or nb==0: return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a),a,0.0); b=np.where(np.isfinite(b),b,0.0)
    size=na+nb-1
    fft_size=1 << (size-1).bit_length()
    A=np.fft.rfft(a,fft_size); B=np.fft.rfft(b,fft_size)
    return np.fft.irfft(A*B, fft_size)[:size]
```

`normalize_seq`:
```
def normalize_seq(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size==0: return a
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
    s=float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15:
       return np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64)/a.size
    return a/s
```

`project_simplex` as above. Need if v has nonfinite: v=np.where.

`quick_score`:
```
def quick_score(a):
    a=normalize_seq(a)
    if a.size==0: return float('inf')
    c=fast_convolve(a)
    if c.size==0: return float('inf')
    return float(2.0*a.size*np.max(c))
```

`try_candidate`:
```
def try_candidate(a, best_a, best_score, label=''):
    if a is None:
       return best_a,best_score
    a=normalize_seq(a)
    if a.size==0:
       return best_a,best_score
    seq=[float(x) for x in a]
    score=float(evaluate_sequence(seq))
    if np.isfinite(score) and score < best_score - 1e-12:
       print(f"NEW BEST {label}: {score:.6f} n={a.size}")
       return a, score
    return best_a, best_score
```

try: score=float(evaluate_sequence(seq))
except Exception: score=float('inf')
```
Good.

def add_seed_info(infos, label, a):

    qs=quick_score(a)
    if np.isfinite(qs):
       infos.append((label, a, float(qs)))

`make_alpha_tail_seed` and `make_tilted_tail_seed`.
```
def make_alpha_tail_seed(n, alpha=0.5):
    if n <=0: return np.array([])
    if not np.isfinite(alpha) or alpha <= 1e-8: alpha=0.2
    if alpha > 1.5: alpha=1.5 # allow? recurrence may >
    a=np.empty(n,dtype=np.float64); a[0]=1.0
    prev=1.0
    for i in range(1,n):
       prev = prev * ((i - 1) + alpha) / float(i)
       a[i]=prev
    return normalize_seq(np.maximum(a,0))
```
For alpha>1, prev grows, normalize okay but could overflow if n large and alpha>1? We only use <=1. For safety if alpha >1, use exp tail? But not. Use if alpha>1: return uniform maybe. Let's restrict:
```
if alpha <=0: alpha=0.05
if alpha >=1.0: a=np.ones(n)/n
else recurrence
```
For alpha=0.999, recurrence okay. If alpha=1 exactly uniform.

`make_tilted_tail_seed`:
```
def make_tilted_tail_seed(n, alpha=0.5, tilt=0.0):
    a=make_alpha_tail_seed(n,alpha)
    if n<=1: return a
    x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    w=1.0 + float(tilt)*x
    w=np.maximum(w, 1e-12)
    return normalize_seq(a*w)
```

`make_exponential_seed`, `make_sine_seed`, `make_random_antisy_seed`, `project_complement`, `interpolate_to_length`.

`interpolate_to_length` as previous but ensure nonnegative and normalize.
```
def interpolate_to_length(a, new_n):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    old=a.size
    if old==new_n: return normalize_seq(a)
    if old==0: return np.ones(new_n)/new_n
    x_old=np.linspace(0,1,old); x_new=np.linspace(0,1,new_n)
    out=np.interp(x_new,x_old,a)
    return normalize_seq(out)
```

`smooth_gradient_from_conv`, `smooth_descent`, `coordinate_descent_pass`, `suggested_pairs`, `transfer_polish`, `lp_direction`, `lp_polish`, `combined_polish`, `propose_candidate`.

Need ensure in `coordinate_descent_pass`, `np.dot` for recurrence not used. Top loops in Python for n=2048 okay but if seed many n=2048*5=10k multiplications, trivial.

Let's write `coordinate_descent_pass` carefully with no mistakes.
Use variable `current_obj` updated. At end if recompute, use best_a if best_obj < current_obj? Actually after loop, `a` may have improved but `best_a` is best found; we should return best_a and recompute. If no improvement, best_a=a initial. Return best_a. But if a has different score slightly lower due to recompute? We can recompute best_a. Good.
```
def coordinate_descent_pass(a0, max_time=30.0, grid_points=14, top_k=8, max_candidates=28, recompute=True):
    a=normalize_seq(a0)
    n=a.size; L=2*n-1
    if n==0: return a,float('inf')
    conv=fast_convolve(a)
    current_obj=float(np.max(conv))
    best_obj=current_obj
    best_a=a.copy()
    t0=time.monotonic()
    if n > 1200:
       grid_points = min(grid_points, 8)
       top_k = min(top_k, 4)
       max_candidates = min(max_candidates, 12)
    for j in range(n):
       if time.monotonic() - t0 > max_time: break
       xold=float(a[j])
       s=1.0 - xold
       if s < 1e-8: continue
       b=a.copy(); b[j]=0.0; b=b/s
       D=np.zeros(L)
       st=j; en=min(j+n, L)
       if en>st: D[st:en]=b[:en-st]
       E=np.zeros(L)
       ej=2*j
       if ej < L: E[ej]=1.0
       B=(conv - xold*xold*E - 2.0*xold*s*D)/(s*s)
       B=np.maximum(B,0.0)
       A=E - 2.0*B
       Bc=2.0*(D - B)
       Cc=B
       xs_list=[]
       if grid_points > 0:
           xs_list.extend(np.linspace(0.0,1.0,int(grid_points)).tolist())
       xs_list.extend([xold,0.0,1.0])
       if top_k > 0 and L > 0:
          K=min(int(top_k), L)
          if K == L:
             top=np.arange(L)
          else:
             top=np.argpartition(conv, -K)[-K:]
          for p in top:
             p=int(p)
             if abs(A[p]) > 1e-15:
                r=-Bc[p]/(2.0*A[p])
                if r >= -1e-12 and r <= 1.0+1e-12:
                   xs_list.append(float(np.clip(r,0.0,1.0)))
          m=len(top)
          for ii in range(m):
             p=int(top[ii])
             for jj in range(ii+1,m):
                q=int(top[jj])
                da=A[p]-A[q]; db=Bc[p]-Bc[q]; dc=Cc[p]-Cc[q]
                if abs(da) > 1e-14:
                   disc=db*db - 4.0*da*dc
                   if disc >= -1e-14:
                      if disc < 0.0: disc=0.0
                      sq=np.sqrt(disc)
                      r1=(-db - sq)/(2.0*da)
                      r2=(-db + sq)/(2.0*da)
                      for r in (r1,r2):
                         if r >= -1e-12 and r <= 1.0+1e-12 and len(xs_list) < 200:
                            xs_list.append(float(np.clip(r,0.0,1.0)))
                elif abs(db) > 1e-14:
                   r=-dc/db
                   if r >= -1e-12 and r <= 1.0+1e-12 and len(xs_list) < 200:
                      xs_list.append(float(np.clip(r,0.0,1.0)))
       xs=np.array(xs_list,dtype=np.float64)
       xs=xs[(xs>=0.0)&(xs<=1.0)]
       if xs.size==0: continue
       xs=np.sort(xs)
       manual unique:
       uniq=[]
       prev_val=None
       for val in xs:
          if prev_val is None or val - prev_val > 1e-8:
             uniq.append(float(val)); prev_val=float(val)
       xs=np.array(uniq)
       if xs.size > max_candidates:
          # select evenly from sorted plus endpoints/xold
          idx=np.linspace(0, xs.size-1, max_candidates).astype(int)
          xs=xs[idx]
          xs=np.concatenate((xs, np.array([0.0,1.0,xold])))
          xs=np.sort(xs)
          # unique again
          uniq=[]; prev_val=None
          for val in xs:
             if prev_val is None or val-prev_val>1e-8: uniq.append(float(val)); prev_val=float(val)
          xs=np.array(uniq)
          if xs.size > max_candidates: xs=xs[:max_candidates]
       best_val=current_obj; best_x=xold
       chunk_size=max(1, 200000 // max(1,L))
       for start in range(0, xs.size, chunk_size):
          chunk=xs[start:start+chunk_size]
          ox=1.0 - chunk
          vals=(ox*ox)[:,None]*B + (2.0*chunk*ox)[:,None]*D + (chunk*chunk)[:,None]*E
          m=vals.max(axis=1)
          idx_local=int(np.argmin(m))
          if m[idx_local] < best_val:
             best_val=float(m[idx_local]); best_x=float(chunk[idx_local])
       if best_val < current_obj - 1e-13 and 0.0 < best_x < 1.0:
          refine = np.linspace(max(0.0, best_x-0.05), min(1.0, best_x+0.05), 16)
          refine = np.concatenate((refine, np.array([best_x])))
          ox=1.0-refine
          vals=(ox*ox)[:,None]*B + (2.0*refine*ox)[:,None]*D + (refine*refine)[:,None]*E
          m=vals.max(axis=1)
          idx_ref=int(np.argmin(m))
          if m[idx_ref] < best_val:
             best_val=float(m[idx_ref]); best_x=float(refine[idx_ref])
       if best_val < current_obj - 1e-14 and abs(best_x - xold) > 1e-12:
          a_new=a.copy()
          if best_x <= 1e-15:
             a_new[j]=0.0
          elif best_x >= 1.0 - 1e-15:
             a_new[:] = 0.0; a_new[j]=1.0
          else:
             a_new[j]=best_x
             mask=np.ones(n,dtype=bool); mask[j]=False
             a_new[mask] *= (1.0 - best_x) / s
             a_new=np.maximum(a_new,0.0)
             ss=float(a_new.sum())
             if ss > 1e-15:
                a_new = a_new/ss
          conv_new=(1.0-best_x)*(1.0-best_x)*B + (2.0*best_x*(1.0-best_x))*D + (best_x*best_x)*E
          conv_new=np.maximum(conv_new,0.0)
          new_obj=float(np.max(conv_new))
          if new_obj < best_obj - 1e-15:
             best_obj=new_obj; best_a=a_new.copy()
          a=a_new
          conv=conv_new
          current_obj=new_obj
    a=normalize_seq(best_a)
    conv=fast_convolve(a)
    obj=float(np.max(conv))
    return a, obj
```
A concern: In the candidate cap selection, we take evenly from sorted unique; this may drop xold if not in endpoints? We add xold after. Good. But if xs.size > max_candidates, taking evenly sorted (biased lower) plus xold may drop high-intersection roots. Could instead select candidates based on proximity to xold: compute distances and take neighbors. But fine. Maybe better: keep grid uniformly spaced by default because x_sorted cap even gives same. Intersections may be lost. To preserve, we can before cap create a base grid `np.linspace(0,1,12)` and add intersections closest to xold. But code already caps. Maybe modify:
- Set max_candidates 28. Top pairs 8 => up to 28 roots + grid 14 => ~50. Cap drops about half sorted. This could lose important. Instead, choose to keep grid 12 plus top roots until cap. Let's do:
  * After unique, if size > max_candidates: We can prioritize 0,1,xold, evenly spaced grid, and then include points with largest deviation from grid? Simpler: Use `selected = np.linspace(0,1,min(12,max_candidates))` plus xold, then for sorted xs, add those not close to selected until max. This preserves roots while keeping coverage.
  Implementation no lambda:
```
if xs.size > max_candidates:
   base=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,min(12,max_candidates))
   selected=[float(v) for v in base]
   selected.extend([0.0,1.0,float(xold)])
   for val in xs:
      if len(selected) >= max_candidates: break
      # check if val close to any selected? O(M^2) small
      close=False
      for sv in selected:
         if abs(val-sv) < 1e-5:
            close=True; break
      if not close:
         selected.append(float(val))
   xs=np.array(selected); xs=np.sort(xs); unique; if xs.size>max_candidates: xs=xs[:max_candidates]
```
This may add roots in order sorted (low first); but okay. We need ensure not too slow; 100*30 comparisons per coordinate ~3k, fine. Let's implement simpler to not overcomplicate. But code length okay.

Actually we can avoid cap by limiting initial list size. Instead of adding all roots, we can stop adding when len(xs_list) > max_candidates*2? Still need select. `transfer_polish` E at i+j might coincide with 2i or 2j if i=j, but i≠j. If i+j==2i only j=i no. If i+j==2j no. So no overlapping. But if i+j index same? no. Use `+=` safe.

`combined_polish`: Need pass `seed` to phases. It should not use `try_candidate` (to avoid evaluate calls). It optimizes internal maxconv for fixed n. Good.
Pseudo code with explicit if chain. We should be careful to handle if `use_coord` false.
```
def combined_polish(a0, time_limit, verbose=False, seed=123):
    a=normalize_seq(a0); n=a.size
    best_a=a.copy()
    conv=fast_convolve(a); best_obj=float(conv.max())
    t0=time.monotonic(); phase=0; no_imp=0
    use_coord = n <= 1100
    use_lp = SCIPY_OPT_OK and (n <= 800)
    while time.monotonic()-t0 < time_limit:
       rem = time_limit - (time.monotonic()-t0)
       if rem < 8: break
       old=best_obj
       # choose action based on phase
       if phase % 10 == 0:
          alloc=min(20.0, rem)
          a_tmp,obj=smooth_descent(best_a, alloc, lr=0.006, mom=0.88, beta_start=2.0, beta_end=200.0, seed=seed+phase)
       elif phase %10 == 1 and use_coord:
          alloc=min(45.0, rem)
          a_tmp,obj=coordinate_descent_pass(best_a, alloc, grid_points=14, top_k=8, max_candidates=28)
       elif phase %10 == 2:
          alloc=min(35.0, rem)
          a_tmp,obj=transfer_polish(best_a, alloc, grid_points=18, rng_seed=seed+phase*7)
       elif phase %10 == 3:
          alloc=min(20.0, rem)
          a_tmp,obj=smooth_descent(best_a, alloc, lr=0.003, mom=0.92, beta_start=50.0, beta_end=3000.0, seed=seed+phase*13)
       elif phase %10 == 4:
          if use_lp and rem > 120:
             alloc=min(60.0, rem)
             a_tmp,obj=lp_polish(best_a, alloc, seed=seed+phase*17, max_solves=2)
          else:
             alloc=min(25.0, rem)
             a_tmp,obj=transfer_polish(best_a, alloc, grid_points=12, rng_seed=seed+phase*19)
       elif phase %10 == 5 and use_coord:
          alloc=min(30.0, rem)
          a_tmp,obj=coordinate_descent_pass(best_a, alloc, grid_points=10, top_k=6, max_candidates=20)
       elif phase %10 == 6:
          alloc=min(25.0, rem)
          a_tmp,obj=transfer_polish(best_a, alloc, grid_points=14, rng_seed=seed+phase*23)
       elif phase %10 == 7:
          # noise + smooth
          rng=np.random.default_rng(seed+phase*29)
          scale=0.003 * max(0.3, 1.0 - 0.02*no_imp)
          a_noise=project_simplex(best_a + rng.standard_normal(n)*scale)
          alloc=min(25.0, rem)
          a_tmp,obj=smooth_descent(a_noise, alloc, lr=0.004, mom=0.9, beta_start=10.0, beta_end=1000.0, seed=seed+phase*31)
       else:
          alloc=min(15.0, rem)
          a_tmp,obj=smooth_descent(best_a, alloc, lr=0.002, mom=0.95, beta_start=100.0, beta_end=10000.0, seed=seed+phase*37)
       if np.isfinite(obj) and obj < best_obj - 1e-15:
          best_obj=float(obj)
          best_a=normalize_seq(a_tmp)
          no_imp=0
          if verbose:
             print(f"  combined polish phase {phase}: obj={best_obj:.12f}")
       else:
          no_imp += 1
       phase += 1
    a=normalize_seq(best_a)
    conv=fast_convolve(a)
    obj=float(conv.max())
    return a, obj
```
If `use_coord` false, phase 1 falls through? In if/elif, if phase%10==1 and use_coord false, it will not execute that branch, but elif chain continues to next? In Python if/elif, if condition false, it checks next condition. So phase 1 will go to phase%10==2? No, condition `elif phase % 10 == 2`: if phase=1, false; eventually else (phase%10 not matching) executes smooth. So okay. But if phase=5 and use_coord false, condition phase%10==5 and use_coord false, then phase%10==6 false, etc else. Good.
Need ensure every branch assigns `a_tmp,obj`. Yes else.

`lp_polish` may be slow; in combined only if rem>120 and max_solves2. Good.

```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800):
    deadline = time.monotonic() + max(120.0, float(budget_s) - 60.0)
    parent = None
    raw = globals().get('height_sequence_1', None)
    if raw is not None:
       try: ...
       except: parent=None
    if parent is None:
       parent = make_alpha_tail_seed(768, 0.5)
    best_a = normalize_seq(parent)
    best_score = float(evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in best_a]))
    if not np.isfinite(best_score):
       best_score = quick_score(best_a)
    print(f"Initial: n={best_a.size}, score={best_score:.6f}")
    base_n = best_a.size
    scan_ns = [base_n]
    if base_n != 1024: scan_ns.append(1024)
    # quick param scan
    quick_best_qs = float('inf')
    quick_param = (base_n,0.5,0.0)
    for nscan in scan_ns:
       alphas = [0.35,0.4,0.45,0.5,0.55,0.6,0.65,0.7,0.8,0.9,1.0]
       tilts = [-0.1,0.0,0.1,0.2]
       for alpha in alphas:
          for tilt in tilts:
             a = make_tilted_tail_seed(nscan, alpha, tilt)
             qs = quick_score(a)
             if np.isfinite(qs):
                if qs < quick_best_qs:
                   quick_best_qs = qs; quick_param=(nscan,alpha,tilt)
                if qs < best_score - 1e-8:
                   best_a,best_score = try_candidate(a,best_a,best_score,f"scan_{nscan}_{alpha}_{tilt}")
    if time.monotonic() > deadline - 300: reserve? Actually if quick scan found best, still need polish maybe no time? deadline has plenty.
    # polish current best
    now=time.monotonic(); rem=deadline-now
    if rem > 100:
       alloc = min(350.0, rem - 100.0)
       if alloc > 20:
          print("Polishing current best")
          a_tmp,obj = combined_polish(best_a, alloc, verbose=True, seed=seed)
          best_a,best_score = try_candidate(a_tmp,best_a,best_score,"polish_best")
    # build seed infos
    seed_infos=[]
    nq, aq, tq = quick_param
    for delta in (-0.05,0.0,0.05):
       add_seed_info(seed_infos, f"alpha{nq}_{aq+delta}_{tq}", make_tilted_tail_seed(nq, aq+delta, tq))
    # add base_n alpha seeds
    for alpha in [0.45,0.5,0.55,0.6]:
       add_seed_info(seed_infos, f"base_{base_n}_{alpha}", make_alpha_tail_seed(base_n, alpha))
    # add larger tail
    n_large = max(1024, min(2048, int(base_n * 1.33)))
    if n_large != base_n:
       for alpha in [aq, 0.5]:
          add_seed_info(seed_infos, f"large_{n_large}_{alpha}", make_alpha_tail_seed(n_large, alpha))
    # sine/random
    add_seed_info(seed_infos, f"sine_{base_n}", make_sine_seed(base_n,1,0.8,0.0))
    add_seed_info(seed_infos, f"rand_{base_n}", make_random_antisy_seed(base_n,8,0.9,seed+1))
    add_seed_info(seed_infos, f"comp_best", project_complement(best_a))
    # if no seeds, add
    if not seed_infos:
       add_seed_info(seed_infos, "tail", make_alpha_tail_seed(base_n,0.5))
    seed_infos.sort(key=seed_score_key)
    # limit to 8, but ensure maybe sorted by quick score. We can also skip seeds whose quick score far worse? Not.
    seed_infos = seed_infos[:8]
    for idx, item in enumerate(seed_infos):
       label = item[0]; a_seed=item[1]
       now=time.monotonic(); rem=deadline-now
       if rem < 120: break
       if idx == 0:
          alloc = min(180.0, rem - 120.0)
       elif idx < 3:
          alloc = min(120.0, rem - 120.0)
       else:
          alloc = min(60.0, rem - 120.0)
       if alloc < 20: break
       print(f"Polish seed {label} quick={item[2]:.6f} alloc={alloc:.0f}")
       a_tmp,obj = combined_polish(a_seed, alloc, verbose=False, seed=seed + 1000 + idx*17)
       best_a,best_score = try_candidate(a_tmp,best_a,best_score,label)
    # final intensive on best
    now=time.monotonic(); rem=deadline-now
    if rem > 80 and best_a.size <= 1100:
       alloc = min(180.0, rem - 80.0)
       if alloc > 20:
          a_tmp,obj=coordinate_descent_pass(best_a, alloc, grid_points=16, top_k=10, max_candidates=32)
          best_a,best_score=try_candidate(a_tmp,best_a,best_score,"final_cd")
    now=time.monotonic(); rem=deadline-now
    if rem > 70:
       alloc = min(90.0, rem - 70.0)
       if alloc > 15:
          a_tmp,obj=transfer_polish(best_a, alloc, grid_points=24, rng_seed=seed+777)
          best_a,best_score=try_candidate(a_tmp,best_a,best_score,"final_transfer")
    now=time.monotonic(); rem=deadline-now
    if rem > 50:
       alloc = min(60.0, rem - 50.0)
       if alloc > 10:
          a_tmp,obj=smooth_descent(best_a, alloc, lr=0.002, mom=0.95, beta_start=100.0, beta_end=30000.0, seed=seed+991)
          best_a,best_score=try_candidate(a_tmp,best_a,best_score,"final_smooth")
    if SCIPY_OPT_OK and best_a.size <= 800:
       now=time.monotonic(); rem=deadline-now
       if rem > 150:
          alloc=min(80.0, rem-50.0)
          if alloc > 30:
             a_tmp,obj=lp_polish(best_a, alloc, seed=seed+2023, max_solves=3)
             best_a,best_score=try_candidate(a_tmp,best_a,best_score,"final_lp")
    # final verify
    best_a = normalize_seq(best_a)
    try:
       final_score = float(evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in best_a]))
    except Exception:
       final_score = quick_score(best_a)
    if not np.isfinite(final_score):
       final_score = quick_score(best_a)
    print(f"FINAL SCORE: {final_score:.6f} n={best_a.size}")
    return [float(x) for x in best_a]
```

`project_simplex`:
```
def project_simplex(v):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = v.size
    if n == 0:
        return v
    if n == 1:
        return np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)
    v = np.where(np.isfinite(v), v, 0.0)
    u = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cssv = np.cumsum(u)
    cond = u * np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=np.float64) > (cssv - 1.0)
    if not cond.any():
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / n
    rho = int(np.nonzero(cond)[0][-1])
    theta = (cssv[rho] - 1.0) / (rho + 1.0)
    out = v - theta
    out = np.maximum(out, 0.0)
    s = out.sum()
    if s <= 1e-15:
       return np.ones(n)/n
    return out / s
```
The standard formula returns sum1 exactly; extra normalization okay.

`smooth_gradient_from_conv`: Need if conv length not 2n-1? assume.
```
def smooth_gradient_from_conv(a, conv, beta):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    conv=np.asarray(conv,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n=a.size; L=2*n-1
    if conv.size < L:
       conv=np.concatenate([conv, np.zeros(L-conv.size)])
    else:
       conv=conv[:L]
    if beta <= 1e-6:
       w=np.full(L, 1.0/L)
    else:
       maxb=float(np.max(conv))
       ex=np.exp(np.float64(beta) * (conv - maxb))
       s=float(ex.sum())
       if s <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(s):
          w=np.zeros(L); w[int(np.argmax(conv))] = 1.0
       else:
          w=ex/s
    c=conv_filter(a, w[::-1])
    if c.size < 2*n-1: # slice safe
       tmp=np.zeros(2*n-1); tmp[:c.size]=c; c=tmp
    grad = 2.0 * c[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]
    grad = grad - np.mean(grad)
    return grad
```
If a.size=0? not.
